## Status icons — the canonical definitions

These six are the ENTIRE vocabulary. `scripts/uat-audit.py` fails the build on any
verdict glyph outside this set, because an uncounted glyph is silently dropped by
`uat-backfill.py` and then reads downstream as "never walked" — which turns an
unreadable verdict into a missing pass. Four rows carried a stray `◑` that way, and
`⏳` dropped thirteen more, before the guard existed.

| icon | name | means | what moves it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `✅` | ⏳ | Every clause in the test case was measured on the CURRENT env and held, with a control showing the check could have come out the other way. | Nothing — until the env is replaced or the clause changes, when it returns to ☐. |
| `❌` | FAIL | Measured on the current env and it did NOT hold. A named, reproducible defect. This is the honest home for anything undelivered — a feature that has not shipped is a FAIL, not a block. | A fix, deployed, then re-walked. |
| `⚠️` | PARTIAL | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict.** Some clauses proven, others not. A test case whose clause does not fully hold is a FAILING test case, so a row may not be REPORTED in this state: resolve it to ✅ or ❌ before a convergence cycle is captured. It exists so a walk-in-progress can be saved mid-flight. | Closing the named residual (→ ✅) or accepting it as the failure it is (→ ❌). |
| `⛔` | SUPERSEDED | The test case cannot be answered as written — its subject no longer exists, or it asserts a value the schema forbids. **Not** a parking space for hard work. | Retire-and-REPLACE, one-for-one, recorded in `uat-retirements.csv`. |
| `☐` | NOTRUN | Never walked on this env. Includes a freshly replaced clause, which must NOT inherit the retired row's verdict. | Walking it. |
| `⏳` | PENDING | **Working state only — never a terminal verdict**, same rule as PARTIAL. Written by `scripts/reset-uat.py` when a new env flushes prior evidence, to mark awaiting-re-walk as distinct from never-walked. A row may not be REPORTED in this state: it is outside the PASS/FAIL vocabulary a reader counts, so a row left here is invisible rather than merely undecided — 3 rows were uncounted that way. | Re-walking on the live env, then ✅ or ❌. |

**The denominator is STONE at 286.** Retiring a row always carries a replacement so
the count never moves; `scripts/uat-snapshot.py` refuses any other total. A
floating denominator makes every percentage incomparable across cycles, and it has
drifted before.

**Evidence is per-env.** A new Sovereign flushes ALL prior evidence, so the score
resets to near-zero on every fresh prov (measured: 133 green 2026-07-30 → 25 on
2026-08-03). A drop across an env boundary is the ledger working; a drop WITHIN one
env is a regression. `scripts/uat-drift-guard.py` refuses a ✅ row that cites a
wiped env.

**Env:** live omantel.biz Sovereign, dep `91dc05917e44d1c1`, 2-region converged (region-a 62/62 HRs · region-b 56/56 HRs, me-east-215-a + me-east-215-b). All verdicts below are against THIS env unless a row carries its own labeled prior-env evidence.

**The single decisive gate:** kom4dc EIP quota is **10** (free 3); a fresh post-fix 2-region prov needs **6** EIPs — a HARD WALL with zero orphans to reclaim. Raising the quota **10 → ≥16** would unblock the 7 roll-gated tickets in one fresh prov. The remaining 6 are Anthropic-credential-walled (2), a real remaining build (1), or destructive-walk-gated (2, need a throwaway env [itself EIP-walled] or founder clearance).

BOTH clauses fail, and the DNS half passing is precisely what hides it. **Clause 1 — resolves in both regions: NAME yes, SERVICE no.** All three `-mesh-rw` Services exist in both regions and DNS answers in both (A: `shared-pg-mesh-rw` → 10.96.244.90, `-b-` → 10.96.97.178, `-c-` → 10.96.47.49; B: 10.97.130.92 / 10.97.167.121 / 10.97.1.131). Dialed for real: from `harbor/harbor-nginx` in region B all three `…-mesh-rw:5432` give **curl exit 7** (never connected); from `grafana/grafana` in region A all three give **exit 28** (connected, then idle-timeout waiting on the postgres wire, which is correct for a TCP probe). EXIT-CODE CONTROL taken in the same shell so the two codes are not assumed: a known-live port (portal:8080) → 28, a known-dead port (portal:9999) → 7. Region B is on the dead side. The Services carry `service.cilium.io/global: "true"` + `service.cilium.io/affinity: local`, so the clustermesh global-service is NOT failing region B over to region A's primary. **Clause 2 — streaming replication is live: NOT CONFIGURED AT ALL.** `spec.replica` is **null** on the shared-pg Cluster in BOTH regions and each declares `instances: 1` — there is no replica cluster and no source, so there is no CNPG streaming replication to be live; region B's copy is an independent primary that never came up (`phase: Setting up primary`, `Ready=False`, `ConsistentSystemID=False`). **MECHANISM** (shared with R13): `shared-data/shared-pg-1-initdb` in region B is `CreateContainerConfigError` because its `APP_USERNAME` env resolves `secret shared-pg-harbor`, which does not exist in that region — 562 image-pull retries over 122m, initdb never runs, the primary never initialises, so the `-mesh-rw` selector `cnpg.io/instanceRole=primary` matches nothing. |

Region A is clean throughout, which is the control: the same charts converge when the Secret is present. |

WHY PARTIAL, two live gaps: (a) the door only answers ~50% of the time — pinned to 212.72.24.82, console.acme-corp 5/10 200, console.delta-corp 4/10, console.walk-two 4/10, rest ERR_CONNECTION_RESET, while console.hw290.omani.works on the SAME IP is 10/10 200 and raw TCP 212.72.24.82:443 is 10/10 OK (delta-corp needed an in-run retry before it loaded); SNI-selective failure of the *.<orgslug> wildcard listeners on Gateway kube-system/cilium-gateway-console. (b) console.gamma-corp.omani.homes does not resolve at all (NXDOMAIN) and has no gateway listener — that Organization has no console door whatsoever (it is also the CR-less Org: organizations.orgs.openova.io/gamma-corp NotFound). ‖ —  ‖ 2026-08-07 SOURCE ADJUDICATION of gap (a), and it REFUTES the hypothesis this row's own evidence invites. The obvious read of 'region-A carries console-https-uatco, region-B does not' is 'the org-controller runs single-region and never writes region-B'. That is WRONG, and this row's own text contains the counter-evidence: region-B's cilium-gateway-console carries 8 listeners INCLUDING per-Org ones for *.r17probe.omani.homes. A controller that could not reach region-B could not have written those either. So region-B IS reachable and HAS been written; what it holds is a per-Org listener set from an EARLIER Org that was never updated for uatco and never cleaned up for r17probe. Anyone fixing this by 'making the controller multi-region' would be fixing a mechanism that is already there. WHAT THE SOURCE SHOWS. tenant_console_tls.go appends the console-https-<slug>/console-http-<slug> pair idempotently to the SHARED kube-system/cilium-gateway-console; teardown IS wired - teardownTenantNetworking (tenant_networking_teardown.go:112) is called from organization_controller.go:455 and is held open by the dedicated TenantNetworkingFinalizer precisely so the cascade cannot be skipped when no other finalizer is present (#4459). So neither the write nor the delete path is missing code. The r17probe listener surviving an Org whose API record reads state='deleted' is therefore NOT a teardown-logic bug - it is #5426 (Organization delete never runs the finalizer cascade: no REST route, no UI action, so the CR is never deleted and the finalizer never fires). The listener outlives the Org because nothing ever asked it to die. CLASSIFICATION: gap (a) belongs to the #5635 family (single-region resource behind a two-region shared EIP, ~50% fresh-TCP failure) - same mechanism, applied to the console listener instead of the app namespace - and the orphan half is #5426. Not filed as a new issue; a third ticket for a mechanism twice-filed would inflate the backlog without adding a fix. NOTE THE MEASUREMENT DISCIPLINE THIS ROW ALREADY GOT RIGHT and that made the adjudication possible: fresh-TCP curl with --no-keepalive. Under HTTP/2 the browser pins one backend and the same host reads 10/10, which is how a 50% split hides. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 PLATFORM REGRESSION, DATED. Same dated cause as row 87 (#5246). The door-collapse claim itself still holds — one catalyst-ui door, BSS a path on it; what fails is 'lands 200', at 5/10. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. A partial is a FAILING test case with some passing parts, and carrying it as its own class let real failures sit in a comfort bucket where no backlog counted them — 27 rows were doing exactly that. NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED HERE and no verdict got worse: the residual named above is the same residual, in the same words, with the same evidence. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. This moves ZERO rows out of green, so it is neither progress nor regression — it is the removal of a hiding place. The row closes when the named residual closes. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: ENV-STATE — needs an environment SHAPE this Sovereign does not have (pre-cutover window, single region, or a destructive prov->wipe cycle). Not a code gap and not re-walkable here. ‖ RE-TAGGED 2026-08-10: NEEDS-CODE -> DEPLOY-GATED. The fix for this row is now MERGED on main as 0439fbea8 (PR #5957). It is not running on hw292 — the deployed images pre-date it — so the verdict correctly stays ❌ and a re-walk here would reproduce it. Verdict and partition are unchanged — only the pointer was wrong. Now enforced pre-merge by scripts/check-uat-fix-sha-citations.py, which fails a citation whose commit is docs-only or whose subject carries a different PR number. ‖ PARTITION CONTRADICTION RESOLVED 2026-08-10 — this cell carried TWO partition labels at once: "PARTITION: ENV-STATE" and, after it, "RE-TAGGED: NEEDS-CODE -> DEPLOY-GATED", which also names a class the row was never in. DEPLOY-GATED is the correct one and ENV-STATE is refuted on the row's own evidence: the residual is not an environment SHAPE this Sovereign lacks, it is that the per-Org console listener pair is written to region A only while the console EIP's backend pool spans both regions, so roughly half of fresh TCP connections reach a region with no listener for that SNI and are reset. That is a code defect with a merged fix — 0439fbea8 (PR #5957) makes every write in tenant_console_tls.go fan over consoleRegionTargets, built from the cutover-secondary-kubeconfigs the #5359 bridge already materializes, and carries the teardown and the #5395 postcondition verifier over the same set. hw292 needs no different shape; it needs that image. ‖ RE-TAGGED 2026-08-11: DEPLOY-GATED -> NEEDS-CODE — the cited fix 0439fbea8 (PR #5957) IS in the artifact this env runs. The organization-controller image is 4d1da63 and 0439fbea8 is an ancestor of it, so the row's own warrant — that the deployed images pre-date the fix — is false here. Measured read-only against BOTH region kubeconfigs on 2026-08-11: region A kube-system/cilium-gateway-console carries console-https-hw293walkone and console-https-hw293walktwo, region B carries ONLY the three Sovereign hostname pairs and ZERO per-Org listeners. That is the exact state #5957 exists to prevent, reproduced on top of #5957. ROOT CAUSE READ AT THE EDIT SITE rather than inferred: tenant_console_tls_regions.go builds its region set from the #5359 bridge Secret catalyst/cutover-secondary-kubeconfigs and cross-checks it against kube-system/cilium-clustermesh as an independent witness; on this env NEITHER object exists — clustermesh is established (clustermesh-apiserver 1/1) but publishes clustermesh-apiserver-*-cert instead, and the cutover chart is still installing per #6004 — so the witness declares zero remote regions and the file's own documented single-region path takes over. | 3 | model | [#3687](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3687) [#4546](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/4546) | The voucher-redeem URL opened in an AUTHED OWNER browser session bounces to `/dashboard` CLIENT-side and leaves NO redeem form rendered for the owner (the marketplace is a static build with client-side auth — the #4546 contract written for this row). | hw292-2026-08-10 | ❌ | RETIRED AND REPLACED 2026-08-10, as a formal disposition of the SUPERSEDED backlog rather than a silent edit. Ground: the clause demanded a SERVER redirect; core/marketplace is a STATIC build whose auth is deliberately client-side, so there is no SSR redirect to observe and the assertion contradicts the shipped contract rather than testing it. NOT deleted — the slot is reused so the denominator stays frozen at 286, and the full swap is in docs/ledger/uat-retirements.csv. Reset to ☐ because a NEW clause has never been walked; inheriting the old row's verdict would be the fabrication this ledger exists to stop. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 WALKED ON THE REPLACEMENT CLAUSE - FAILS, and the mechanism is architectural rather than a bug in the page. A live unredeemed voucher was taken from GET /api/v1/org/billing/vouchers/list (26 available, plan m, 50 OMR) and its redeem URL opened in the SAME browser that held the authed owner session. RESULT: the browser stayed on marketplace/redeem/ - redirectCount 0, no bounce to /dashboard - and the STRANGER flow rendered: 1 form, 1 voucher-code input, a 'Sign up to redeem' button, and the voucher-valid copy. The owner identity appears nowhere on the page. SESSION CONTROL, which is what makes this a finding instead of an expired cookie: navigating straight back to the console seconds later returned GET /api/v1/whoami = 200 with email emrah.baysal@openova.io and tier `owner`. The session was live the whole time. MECHANISM, measured not argued: document.cookie is EMPTY on the console's OWN origin, so the catalyst_session cookie is HttpOnly. The marketplace is a separate host running a STATIC build with CLIENT-side auth, and client-side JS cannot read an HttpOnly cookie - so a static marketplace page has no readable signal that the visitor is the owner, on this or any other Sovereign. The owner bounce cannot happen through that path BY CONSTRUCTION, not by oversight. This is the same conclusion the retired clause reached from the opposite direction: the retired version demanded a SERVER redirect, which a static build cannot perform; the replacement demanded a CLIENT bounce, which an HttpOnly session cannot trigger. Both fail for the same underlying reason, so this is a real product gap and not a mis-stated assertion: an owner opening a redeem link is shown a signup form. TO CLOSE: the marketplace needs a readable owner signal - a non-HttpOnly hint cookie scoped to the parent domain, or a whoami call from the marketplace origin with CORS credentials - and the row then asserts the bounce against that. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: NEEDS-CODE — no fix exists yet. This row is engineering work, not a walk; a re-walk cannot change it. ‖ RE-ADJUDICATED 2026-08-10 — NO FURTHER ACTION, LEFT RED. This slot was already retired-and-replaced today AND then walked on the replacement clause, which failed for a MEASURED architectural reason (an HttpOnly session cookie plus a static marketplace origin, with a live whoami=200 as the session control). A second rewrite would be re-wording a clause that has already been re-worded once and then tested; the residual is product work, not ledger work. ‖ RE-TAGGED 2026-08-11: NEEDS-CODE -> WALKABLE NOW — the fix EXISTS, is purpose-built for this row, and is SERVED on this env. #5965 (`f25c4d7dc`, 2026-08-10T09:32Z) added `products/catalyst/bootstrap/api/internal/handler/session_hint.go`: a non-HttpOnly `catalyst_session_hint` companion set alongside every `catalyst_session` and scoped to the same `sessionCookieDomain` — literally the readable owner signal this cell's own TO-CLOSE line asks for, and its file header names UAT row 3. Consumed client-side by `core/marketplace/src/lib/sessionHint.ts`, `redeemDestination.ts` and `returningVisitor.ts`, with `redeem.astro` checking the hint BEFORE the server probe. RENDERED-ARTIFACT PROOF rather than a source grep, because this storefront is a static build: `GET https://marketplace.hw293.omantel.biz/redeem/` returns 200 and the SERVED HTML contains `catalyst_session_hint` three times. Merged AND deployed AND never re-walked since, so a walk on THIS env is what decides it. |

FIX MERGED 2026-08-07, NOT YET DELIVERED (PR #5801, commit d94b8a2a1): `git merge-base --is-ancestor d94b8a2a1 fad88bd` -> NO, so the running catalyst-ui/catalyst-api (built 2026-08-02) provably predates it and this row CANNOT flip on the current image. ROOT CAUSE, corrected from the ticket: #5434 diagnosed this as 'the backend never inverts the producer's parameters.databases[].consumer map'. That is not what was wrong. The inverter ALREADY EXISTED and was correct — dependsOnFromHRGraph reads the HelmRelease's Flux dependsOn graph and resolves each target's declared Context entry — but it ran ONLY inside synthesiseAppFromHelmRelease, which is gated on the Application CR being ABSENT. A bootstrap app like spine-harbor HAS a CR (spec {bootstrap:true, helmRelease:{name:bp-harbor, namespace:flux-system}}), so it took HandleApplicationGet, found no spec.dependsOn, and returned empty while the working resolver sat one branch away. The fix is a fallback that reaches the existing resolver, NOT a second derivation: inverting the producer map was declined deliberately because two ways to compute one edge is how the CR path and the HR path drifted apart into this bug in the first place. Guarded by an AST test on the CALL SITE rather than the helper — the defect was never a wrong resolver, it was a resolver nothing called, which is the shape a behaviour test cannot see. Mutation-verified: deleting the call site, removing the delegation, and stripping the rationale comment each turn a test RED. Re-walk this row after the image rolls. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 VERDICT PROMOTED FROM ⚠️ — deploy-gate CONFIRMED by two-way ancestry. FIX: capitalisation is now OPT-IN per call site (enumValue prop); Slug and Owner do not set it. GUARD, both directions mutation-verified: restoring the blanket capitalize turns the identifier test RED, stripping capitalize from every field turns the CONTROL test RED. So the walk's finding - 'the CR and API both say tier=org with the plan carried separately as planSlug=m' - is not a residual at all. ROOT CAUSE SHARPENED 2026-08-07 (source read, no env needed) — the residual is ARCHITECTURAL, not a count bug. The apps grid does not render the Application ESTATE at all; it renders the blueprint CATALOG. AppsPage.tsx:116 builds its card list from `resolveApplications(store.selectedComponents)`, and applicationCatalog.ts:72 iterates BOOTSTRAP_KIT plus the selected component set — i.e. the wizard store's catalog. Application CRs never contribute card IDENTITY. They are layered in only as STATUS: AppsPage.tsx:130-134 comments it exactly — 'Layer in live status from /api/v1/sovereign/apps so the AppCards reflect ground truth'. So the walk's observation (46 bp-* cards, 14 Application CRs, none of them rendering as their own card) is not a discrepancy between two counts; it is one collection being displayed and a different collection being consulted for badges. CONSEQUENCE: this row's assertion ('one card per Application') and the implementation disagree at the design level, so no small patch satisfies it. Making it true means re-keying the grid on the Application list and treating catalog entries as the not-yet-installed remainder — a deliberate UX change, not a bugfix. THIS NEEDS ADJUDICATION, NOT A UNILATERAL REWRITE: /apps currently answers 'what CAN this Sovereign run', and the row asks it to answer 'what IS running'. Both are legitimate products; only the founder should choose. I am deliberately not 'fixing' this by rewriting the grid, because that would silently change an operator surface to satisfy a ledger row. Two spellings of one Org is exactly what this row asserts against. Note this row already recorded it as PERSISTING: an earlier fix removed the literal word 'sovereign' from this path without touching the mismatch underneath, which is why it survived. IT WAS NEVER ONLY COSMETIC. The parent bucket is selected by `org == parentOrg`, where org comes from the openova.io/organization NAMESPACE LABEL, and label values written by the org-controller are CR slugs - DNS-1123, dots impossible. An FQDN-shaped parentOrg could therefore never match anything, so the parent group rendered at 0 cost units on a Sovereign plainly running workloads. That is residual (b): this row's own observation that showback emits the parent at 0 while the treemap Organization layer has no parent node. ONE root cause, TWO symptoms - they were recorded as separate residuals. FIX: route through orgNamespace(), the canonical org->identifier seam (namespace_ensure.go), the same one the self-org CR producer uses. The 'sovereign' fallback for an unresolvable deployment is kept deliberately - it is VISIBLY synthetic, and inventing a plausible-LOOKING slug for an Org we cannot identify would read as a real Organization on the panel. 5 guards, mutation-verified: reverting to the FQDN turns 3 RED; re-deriving inline as strings.ReplaceAll(fqdn,'.','-') turns 2 RED; deleting the reasoning comment turns 1 RED. The AGREEMENT test is the load-bearing one - it compares against spineOrganizationSlug, the producer, NOT a hand-written literal, because a literal lets the two drift the moment either derivation changes. The SEAM test is why the inline mutation exists: ReplaceAll gets hw292.omani.works right and passes a naive behaviour test, then diverges on orgNamespace's dash-run collapse (it fails on a--b..c.example), which is precisely how the two derivations drifted apart to begin with. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite 255s green. NOT CLAIMED: residual NEW (i) - the /apps grid showing 0 of 14 Applications - is #5814. Residual NEW (ii) - GET /api/v1/organizations returning 3 records against 2 Organization CRs - is untouched and still open. ‖ RESIDUAL (ii) FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5833, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292). '3 records where only 2 Organization CRs exist' is mergeOrgResponses keying on Subdomain ALONE. Two holes, both producing a DUPLICATE rather than a miss - which is why the symptom was an inflated count and not a disappearing Org: (1) the CR side ALWAYS sets Subdomain to the slug (orgCRToResponse) while the store side sets whatever the provision record carries, and a custom-domain Org (DomainMode != free-subdomain) legitimately has NO pool subdomain, so one Organization gets two different keys; (2) an EMPTY key skipped dedupe ENTIRELY - both loops appended without registering anything, so a store row with no subdomain and its own CR row both landed. FIX: two rows are the same Org when they match on EITHER axis (OrganizationID or Subdomain), both registered on insert, axes namespaced id:/sub: so a subdomain equal to another Org's tenant id cannot merge two DIFFERENT Organizations. A row identifiable on neither axis is KEPT but explicitly excluded from dedupe rather than silently treated as deduped. THE CORRECTION WORTH CARRYING: my first cut keyed on OrganizationID with Subdomain as fallback. ALL SIX TARGETED TESTS PASSED. The FULL suite then went red on a pre-existing case - a store record carrying uuid-acme and the CR for the same slug acme have DIFFERENT ids, so id-first split one Organization into two, mirroring the very defect being fixed. Six green targeted tests are not evidence that an invariant holds, only evidence about the six things they check; the 254s full suite is what caught it. That invariant is now pinned by TestMergeOrgResponses_SameSlugDifferentIDsIsOneOrg. 7 guards, mutation-verified 4 ways, plus controls that a too-AGGRESSIVE key would drop the funnel-only rows #4479 added this merge to surface. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite 254s green. STILL OPEN ON THIS ROW: residual (a) identifier mismatch is #5819 (merged), NEW (i) /apps showing 0 of 14 Applications is #5814 (merged) - both deploy-gated; the treemap parent-node half rides on #5819's join repair. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 VERDICT PROMOTED FROM ⚠️ — deploy-gate CONFIRMED by two-way ancestry. The cited fix is verifiably NOT an ancestor of the deployed image, and the CONTROL runs the identical command against a commit that IS one (`<deployed>^` → ancestor, exit 0) and one that is not (origin/main HEAD → exit 1), so the check has been seen to answer both ways. This is a determinate state, not a partial: no source work remains and it closes on the next prov. hw292 cannot receive it — the cutover chart wants 0.1.174, has 0.1.159 applied, and cannot upgrade because the upgrade needs the registry path that is itself broken. Clauses (a),(b),(ii) are deploy-gated; clause (i) inherits row 15's producer wall below and will NOT close on a roll. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. A partial is a FAILING test case with some passing parts, and carrying it as its own class let real failures sit in a comfort bucket where no backlog counted them — 27 rows were doing exactly that. NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED HERE and no verdict got worse: the residual named above is the same residual, in the same words, with the same evidence. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. This moves ZERO rows out of green, so it is neither progress nor regression — it is the removal of a hiding place. The row closes when the named residual closes. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. THAT IS WRONG - the mechanism works, and my test never invoked it. RE-RUN, identical except one step: signed in via PIN, traversed grafana to prime the realm session, dropped ONLY catalyst_session (keeping all four realm cookies AUTH_SESSION_ID, KC_AUTH_SESSION_HASH, KEYCLOAK_IDENTITY, KEYCLOAK_SESSION), CLEARED sessionStorage, reloaded the bare console URL -> lands on /dashboard with ZERO email inputs, signed in as the owner, with freshly minted oidc access/id/refresh tokens. WHAT THE FIRST RUN MEASURED: I had not cleared sessionStorage, so catalyst:authed=1 survived from the initial login while the cookie was gone. router.tsx calls this the 'stale-marker fast path' and notes the layout 'discovers the 401 when the stale-marker fast path has bypassed this gate' - so the app took the fast path, hit a 401 and dropped to the PIN wall WITHOUT attempting silent SSO. I saw the PIN wall and blamed the mechanism; the mechanism never ran. THE ROW STILL FAILS, for a narrower and better reason: a genuine TTL expiry produces exactly the state my first run created by accident - the cookie expires on the server clock while catalyst:authed=1 persists for the tab's lifetime - so a real re-entry hits the fast path, 401s and PIN-walls, while the silent path that demonstrably works never fires. The defect is 'when catalyst:authed is stale relative to the session cookie, the fast path bypasses silent SSO instead of falling back to it', tracked on #5887. Evidence docs/sessions/2026-08-08/hw292-ttl/hw292-silent-reauth-works.png. ‖ hw292-2026-08-08 — FIX WRITTEN AND MERGED THIS SESSION (#5909), delivery blocked by the same source-level freeze as the rest. MECHANISM, from the page's OWN authenticated requests (same guacamole-token header on every one, so this discriminates rather than reflecting an unauthenticated probe): GET /api/session/data/postgresql/connectionGroups/ROOT/tree = 200 and the postgresql-shared twin = 200, but GET /api/session/data/postgresql/self/effectivePermissions = 403 PERMISSION_DENIED and its postgresql-shared twin = 403 — the home controller awaits effectivePermissions and renders the generic ERROR on rejection. NOT A STALE SESSION EITHER: the oidc-gate cookie was deleted browser-side and the bare URL re-entered, producing an identical ERROR on a fully fresh handshake — 3/3 loads. Surface to re-walk when it moves: guacamole.properties on guacamole-server, which sets extension-priority 'header, postgresql' with http-auth-header X-Forwarded-Email, so the session's AuthenticatedUser comes from the header extension while the permissions live in the JDBC extension behind it — named as the surface, NOT asserted as the proven cause. Both are the same structural fact — bootstrap-kit components are HelmReleases WITHOUT Application CRs, so every Application-keyed surface degrades for exactly that set. Fixing one without the other would move a symptom. NOT PATCHING THIS UNILATERALLY: the honest fix is either (a) synthesise Application CRs for bootstrap components so the whole Application-keyed surface works uniformly, or (b) give the Status panel a HelmRelease-backed per-region health path so it stops abstaining. (a) changes the object model; (b) changes what 'Status' means. Both are founder-level design calls, and the row cannot go green until one is chosen. ‖ THE SINGLE REMAINING RESIDUAL IS FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5835, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), and it was the SAME defect in rows 67 and 69. The Status panel reading 'n/a - bootstrap component (HelmRelease, no Application CR)' is not a UI copy choice: HandleApplicationStatus read the Application CR AND NOTHING ELSE, so a bootstrap-kit component (an HR with no companion CR) got a hard 404, while its SIBLING GET /applications/{name} returned a complete synthesised answer (synthesiseAppFromHelmRelease, and since #5827 synthesiseAppFromRuntime). The console reacted the only way it could - TopologyTab.tsx:144 DISABLES the poll entirely for isBootstrap - and printed the n/a string. So one page contradicted itself: this row's own walk measured the header and Overview reading STATUS Ready, from the synthesised detail response, while the Status panel three inches below said there was no status to report. The data existed the whole time; only that endpoint refused to project it. FIX: /status gets the same fallback chain as the detail endpoint, HR first then runtime. It DELEGATES rather than re-deriving - computing a phase from the HelmRelease here would be a THIRD derivation of one component's status, and two was already enough for the detail and status paths to disagree in front of an operator. ORDERING IS GUARDED: runtime-first would reduce a bootstrap component's declared identity to observed Pods on this endpoint while the detail endpoint still reports it - the same disagreement in the other direction. No spec is invented (no CR means no spec.placement; the Topology tab derives real placement from live Pods via .../placement, #3982). 4 guards, mutation-verified: delete the call site; drop the runtime fall-through; flip the ordering. A CLAIM I RETRACTED BEFORE SHIPPING: my first conditions test asserted the key is always PRESENT on the wire. The struct tag says otherwise - conditions carries omitempty, so an empty slice is omitted entirely, on the CR-backed path as much as this one. Callers tolerate an absent key; what they cannot tolerate is null where a list is expected. The stronger assertion would have pinned a contract the existing code does not honour and broken the CR path this change does not touch. NOT CLAIMED: the FE isBootstrap gate is NOT removed - that is a separate change and should follow a live re-walk confirming the endpoint now answers. This row stays partial until then. ROOT CAUSE UNIFIED 2026-08-07 (source read, no env needed) — this row's residual is NOT specific to its app, and it is the SAME cause as rows 19/188. The Status panel string the walk quotes verbatim is emitted at products/catalyst/bootstrap/ui/src/pages/sovereign/AppDetail/TopologyTab.tsx:1045-1047, gated on `isBootstrap`: when the component is a bootstrap-kit HelmRelease with no Application CR, the panel renders 'n/a — bootstrap component (HelmRelease, no Application CR). Live rollout status is tracked via Flux.' unconditionally. So the per-region HEALTH verdict this row asks for is not missing because of anything wrong with grafana or keycloak — the data plane measured GREEN in both regions (pods Running per region, zero matching error lines per region). It is missing because the Application-keyed status surface deliberately declines to speak for bootstrap components, which have no Application CR to speak about. ONE CAUSE, FOUR ROWS: 19 and 188 (the apps grid is keyed on the blueprint CATALOG, so no Application CR renders as its own card) and 67 and 69 (the app-detail Status panel abstains for bootstrap components). Both are the same structural fact — bootstrap-kit components are HelmReleases WITHOUT Application CRs, so every Application-keyed surface degrades for exactly that set. Fixing one without the other would move a symptom. NOT PATCHING THIS UNILATERALLY: the honest fix is either (a) synthesise Application CRs for bootstrap components so the whole Application-keyed surface works uniformly, or (b) give the Status panel a HelmRelease-backed per-region health path so it stops abstaining. (a) changes the object model; (b) changes what 'Status' means. Both are founder-level design calls, and the row cannot go green until one is chosen. ‖ THE SINGLE REMAINING RESIDUAL IS FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5835, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), and it was the SAME defect in rows 67 and 69. The Status panel reading 'n/a - bootstrap component (HelmRelease, no Application CR)' is not a UI copy choice: HandleApplicationStatus read the Application CR AND NOTHING ELSE, so a bootstrap-kit component (an HR with no companion CR) got a hard 404, while its SIBLING GET /applications/{name} returned a complete synthesised answer (synthesiseAppFromHelmRelease, and since #5827 synthesiseAppFromRuntime). The console reacted the only way it could - TopologyTab.tsx:144 DISABLES the poll entirely for isBootstrap - and printed the n/a string. So one page contradicted itself: this row's own walk measured the header and Overview reading STATUS Ready, from the synthesised detail response, while the Status panel three inches below said there was no status to report. The data existed the whole time; only that endpoint refused to project it. FIX: /status gets the same fallback chain as the detail endpoint, HR first then runtime. It DELEGATES rather than re-deriving - computing a phase from the HelmRelease here would be a THIRD derivation of one component's status, and two was already enough for the detail and status paths to disagree in front of an operator. ORDERING IS GUARDED: runtime-first would reduce a bootstrap component's declared identity to observed Pods on this endpoint while the detail endpoint still reports it - the same disagreement in the other direction. No spec is invented (no CR means no spec.placement; the Topology tab derives real placement from live Pods via .../placement, #3982). 4 guards, mutation-verified: delete the call site; drop the runtime fall-through; flip the ordering. A CLAIM I RETRACTED BEFORE SHIPPING: my first conditions test asserted the key is always PRESENT on the wire. The struct tag says otherwise - conditions carries omitempty, so an empty slice is omitted entirely, on the CR-backed path as much as this one. Callers tolerate an absent key; what they cannot tolerate is null where a list is expected. The stronger assertion would have pinned a contract the existing code does not honour and broken the CR path this change does not touch. NOT CLAIMED: the FE isBootstrap gate is NOT removed - that is a separate change and should follow a live re-walk confirming the endpoint now answers. This row stays partial until then. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite rc=0 254s; noted honestly, an immediately prior identical run reported FAIL at 253s with no capturable failure lines, so there is an unidentified flake in the suite and this is ONE clean run, not a verified-stable claim. ‖ hw292-2026-08-10 PROMOTED ⚠️ → ❌ — identical cause to row 67 (`TopologyTab.tsx:144` poll gate + `:1045-1047` hard-rendered n/a string, both still on main). Recorded separately so neither row is closed by a fix that only addresses the other. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement. ‖ hw293-2026-08-10 LIVE READ-ONLY WALK, both regions read through their OWN apiservers. ❌ — keycloak is NOT Healthy/Running in both regions. Region-a: keycloak-0 Running, ready, 0 restarts, and GET /applications/keycloak/status answers phase=Ready with conditions Ready=True and Released=True. Region-b: keycloak-0 is Pending and has never started — kubelet event MountVolume.SetUp failed for volume keycloak-secrets: secret keycloak-database-secret not found, repeated x33 over 51m — alongside SIX keycloak-config-cli job pods in Failed. THE FAILURE IS NOT THE ONE THIS CLAUSE NAMES, and that correction is the substance of this stamp: there is no UnknownHostException and no JGroups DB-host resolution failure, because the pod never reaches the point of resolving a host. It is wedged at volume mount on a Secret that exists in region-a and was never materialised in region-b — the per-region secret-split class, not a DNS fault. Anyone reading the row's wording and debugging JGroups here would be working the wrong layer. Recorded separately from row 67 so that neither row can be closed by a fix addressing only the other, although on this env both trace to this single missing Secret. |

THE ROW'S OWN VACUITY GUARD IS WHAT THE DATABASE SHOWS: guacamole_entity holds exactly two rows — emrah.baysal@openova.io (USER) and sovereign-admins (USER_GROUP) — and guacamole_system_permission grants that group ADMINISTER plus CREATE_CONNECTION, CREATE_CONNECTION_GROUP, CREATE_SHARING_PROFILE, CREATE_USER, CREATE_USER_GROUP. That is the RIGHT to create a connection and not a connection, which the clause declares a FAIL, so the guard fires exactly as written. THE MISSING PRODUCER IS VISIBLE AS A RUNNING OBJECT, not only in source: CronJob guacamole-bp-guacamole-admin-enroll runs every minute and its latest Job completed 4s with the log '[jdbc-seed] db reachable / [jdbc-seed] enrolled 0 user(s) into sovereign-admins'. The only recurring writer the chart ships enrols IDENTITY. Nothing on this Sovereign creates a connection row. NOT A DEPLOY GAP: the platform tier here is healthy — guacamole-server 1/1, guacd 1/1, guacamole-pg-1 1/1, and the guacamole.hw293.omantel.biz HTTPRoute present via oidc-gate — so this is the producer being absent, not a wedged rollout. #5992 removed a fabricated embed URL and did not add an INSERT path; the state it left behind is the state measured here. DELIBERATELY NOT SEEDED BY THIS WALK: a bare INSERT would render a connection that 401s on click, because bp-k8s-ws-proxy requires X-Catalyst-Timestamp + X-Catalyst-HMAC on upgrade and guacd's kubernetes handler cannot emit them — passing the row while the feature stayed broken. The row needs the INSERT path AND that wire, and remains engineering work. |

A first attempt at (A) re-asserted the producer status unconditionally and was caught by the pre-existing TestReconcilerBridge_FailingCron_RedWhileInstallGreen, because a cron leaf status is its latest RUN and not the producer - the shipped fix folds the runs through resolveRunHeadline instead. SCREENSHOT: [📷 row183-openbao-bare-url-notauthorized.png](../../docs/sessions/2026-08-06/hw292-screenshots/row183-openbao-bare-url-notauthorized.png) (hw292-2026-08-06)  ‖ CANDIDATE ROOT CAUSE FOUND + FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5829, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292), and the 08-04-PASS / 08-06-FAIL split on the SAME env is what pointed at it. In the sso-configure reconcile loop the two sys/policies/acl writes were the ONLY writes whose failure was swallowed - `if bao_write ...; then log ...; fi` with NO else - while the auth/oidc/role/operator write SIX LINES BELOW already did `else log WARN; continue`. So a failed policy write did not stop the tick: the role was then written with token_policies=[sso-operator-read], naming a policy that does not exist. OpenBao accepts that role; its tokens carry a dangling policy name and therefore NO capabilities, and every request 403s - including sys/internal/ui/mounts, which is exactly what the UI calls to fill the Secrets Engines panel. That is this row's symptom verbatim: session genuine (final URL /ui/vault/secrets, hasTokenInput=false, identity badge present) while the panel reads 'Not authorized', 3/3 loads, with NO error anywhere because the LOGIN SUCCEEDS. It also explains the date split: the 08-04 walk hit the bare host with no prior session, the 08-06 walk was explicitly POST-SESSION-ESTABLISH, so only the second exercised the OIDC-minted token rather than the bootstrap one. FIX: both policy writes now gate the tick. `continue`, NOT a louder log - the failure mode is ORDERING, a policy must EXIST before a role names it, and writing the role first with the policy 'next tick' leaves a window where the operator is authenticated and powerless. Guard tests/sso-policy-write-gates-tick-5829.sh, mutation-verified (reverting EITHER gate turns it red); it asserts on `continue` INSIDE the extracted write block rather than on the file containing `continue` anywhere (the render has several - a whole-file grep could never go red), opens with a vacuity control proving the script rendered at all, and pins the policy-before-role ORDERING separately because if the role write moves above the policy writes every `continue` becomes decorative. Lockstep 5 sites, bp-openbao 1.2.65->1.2.66 + bp-catalyst-platform 1.4.1329->1.4.1330; check-bootstrap-kit-pin-sync 67 pairs PASS, catalog-seed lockstep 68 checked 0 fail, g117-5-sso-tier1 still PASS. HONEST SCOPE: I could NOT reach hw292's OpenBao to confirm the live token's policy set. The walk's own evidence is consistent with this cause and I found no other path that produces a 403 on that endpoint from a cleanly authenticated session, but this is a SOURCE-DERIVED diagnosis, not a live one, and the row stays partial until re-walked. The fix is unconditionally correct either way: a swallowed write that leaves a role referencing a missing policy is a defect on its own terms. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. A partial is a FAILING test case with some passing parts, and carrying it as its own class let real failures sit in a comfort bucket where no backlog counted them — 27 rows were doing exactly that. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. The apps grid does not render the Application ESTATE at all; it renders the blueprint CATALOG. AppsPage.tsx:116 builds its card list from `resolveApplications(store.selectedComponents)`, and applicationCatalog.ts:72 iterates BOOTSTRAP_KIT plus the selected component set — i.e. the wizard store's catalog. Application CRs never contribute card IDENTITY. They are layered in only as STATUS: AppsPage.tsx:130-134 comments it exactly — 'Layer in live status from /api/v1/sovereign/apps so the AppCards reflect ground truth'. So the walk's observation (46 bp-* cards, 14 Application CRs, none of them rendering as their own card) is not a discrepancy between two counts; it is one collection being displayed and a different collection being consulted for badges. CONSEQUENCE: this row's assertion ('one card per Application') and the implementation disagree at the design level, so no small patch satisfies it. Making it true means re-keying the grid on the Application list and treating catalog entries as the not-yet-installed remainder — a deliberate UX change, not a bugfix. THIS NEEDS ADJUDICATION, NOT A UNILATERAL REWRITE: /apps currently answers 'what CAN this Sovereign run', and the row asks it to answer 'what IS running'. Both are legitimate products; only the founder should choose. I am deliberately not 'fixing' this by rewriting the grid, because that would silently change an operator surface to satisfy a ledger row. Sibling: row 188 (catalyst-api likewise has no card) is the same cause. ‖ THE UI LEG IS FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-08-07 (#5827, PR pending; deploy-gated on hw292). This row's own evidence contained the contradiction and it took a source read to see it: GET .../applications/catalyst-api/placement answers 200 with a correct single Primary target (this row measured that), while GET .../applications/catalyst-api answers 404 - two endpoints on the SAME path prefix disagreeing about whether the component exists. Cause: catalyst-api is a Deployment rendered BY the bp-catalyst-platform HR, so it owns neither an Application CR nor a same-named HelmRelease and HandleApplicationGet's two lookups both miss; the placement endpoint does not care because it derives from LIVE PODS. So the API simultaneously knew the component and denied it existed depending on the suffix, and the console rendered 'App not found - the component catalyst-api is not part of this deployment' for something plainly running in front of the caller. That is exactly why this row's own subject could not be walked in a browser while its backend clause passed. FIX: a THIRD and last fallback, synthesiseAppFromRuntime, after the CR and the HR both miss. ONE IDENTITY PREDICATE, NOT TWO - existence is decided by podBelongsToComponent, the exact function the placement path uses; re-answering the same question a second way IS the defect, not an implementation detail of it. Readiness likewise reuses the existing podIsReady (I wrote one, found the identical function in dashboard.go, deleted mine). FABRICATES NOTHING: no CR means no uid/blueprint/version/parameters/environmentRef, all left empty rather than guessed; runtimeDerived:true marks the whole answer as an OBSERVATION so a consumer can tell 'declares nothing' from 'declaration not fetched' without inferring it from which fields are blank. Phase is strict - Ready requires EVERY matched Pod ready, because rounding one degraded replica of three up to Ready is how a status panel starts lying. ORDERING IS LOAD-BEARING and guarded: the runtime path is LAST, because running it before the HR lookup would strip every bootstrap component's declared blueprint down to the bare observed shape. 4 guards, mutation-verified. ONE CAUGHT ITSELF and is worth recording: the first omitempty mutation SILENTLY DID NOT APPLY - gofmt had realigned the struct field so the replace matched nothing - and the test 'passed'. A mutation that does not apply is not a passing test, it is an unexecuted mutation. Redone against the real text; it then went red as intended. Gates: go vet clean, full handler suite 254s green. NOT FIXED HERE: this does not give bootstrap components Application CRs. Rows 19, 67 and 69 all trace to that same structural absence and are untouched. ‖ NO-PARTIALS DISPOSITION 2026-08-10 — RECLASSIFIED ⚠️ -> ❌. Every clause in a test case must hold for the row to pass; this row's evidence NAMES a clause that does not. A partial is a FAILING test case with some passing parts, and carrying it as its own class let real failures sit in a comfort bucket where no backlog counted them — 27 rows were doing exactly that. NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED HERE and no verdict got worse: the residual named above is the same residual, in the same words, with the same evidence. Only the class changed, from one that reads as 'nearly there' to one that reads as 'does not pass'. This moves ZERO rows out of green, so it is neither progress nor regression — it is the removal of a hiding place. The row closes when the named residual closes. ‖ PARTITION 2026-08-10: DEPLOY-GATED — the fix is MERGED and simply not running on this env. Re-walking here reproduces this same FAIL; it closes on a roll or a fresh prov, not on more measurement. ‖ hw293-2026-08-10 LIVE READ-ONLY WALK. ❌ ON A VACUITY GROUND, and the control is the finding rather than a footnote to it. The literal clause does hold: catalyst-api genuinely IS single-region (one pod, namespace catalyst-system, region-a only; region-b has none), and GET /applications/catalyst-api/placement returns exactly one target, hw-me-east-215-a-rtz-prod role=Primary, with derivedFromRuntime=true and no false multi-region. But this surface cannot report anything else, so the right answer here is not evidence that it was correctly derived. THE CONTROL, taken from each region's OWN apiserver rather than from the console: bp-alloy runs 6 pods in region-a and 6 in region-b, powerdns 7 and 5, cilium 14 and 14, openova-flow 10 and 9 — and the same placement endpoint returns the identical single region-a Primary for alloy and for powerdns, and targets=0 for cilium and for openova-flow. Singleton is therefore the only value this endpoint produces for any component on this Sovereign. ROOT CAUSE, measured: the Sovereign's catalyst-api holds an EMPTY /var/lib/catalyst/kubeconfigs directory — the exact path its own CATALYST_K8SCACHE_KUBECONFIGS_DIR env var names — and every k8scache 'informer synced' log line carries cluster=a0077ba47e3720e5, one cluster, never a second. The console has no credential for region-b, whose apiserver is otherwise entirely healthy and answers 6 nodes to a direct query. So the live defect is the MIRROR of the one this row guards against: not false multi-region, but FALSE SINGLETON on genuinely two-region components. A ✅ here would tell a future reader this surface discriminates single-region apps correctly, which is the opposite of what was measured. CODE/DELIVERY defect on a served connection — not transport, and not region-b degradation, since region-b itself is reachable and healthy. |

THE CHAIN, BOTH ENDS MEASURED. This is precisely the gap the code comment at internal/handler/deployment_handover_export.go:440 anticipates — a missed one-shot handover-export window leaves the chroot's kubeconfig dir empty forever and #3991/#4001 'never address DELIVERY'. UPDATE TO WHAT #6015 RECORDED: that issue observed the directory EMPTY at 13:08; by 15:09 the region-a file had self-registered while the secondary still had not arrived, so the gap is specifically the SECONDARY leg and not first-write. COUPLING WORTH NAMING: the failed status originates in the #6004 cutover component, so one over-size Helm release Secret is also what suppresses secondary-kubeconfig delivery and, through it, multi-region topology.|

VERDICT STAYS ❌, but the reason is now a MEASURED DEFECT ON THIS ROW'S OWN NAMED ENTRY POINT rather than only an unwalked leg. THE ENTRY SURFACE IS HALF-DEAD: https://marketplace.hw293.omantel.biz/redeem/?code=... served only 9 of 20 FRESH-TCP connections; the other 11 returned envoy 'no healthy upstream' (HTTP/1.1 503, server: envoy, body literally 'no healthy upstream'). Sampled with curl --no-keepalive so every request opens a NEW TCP connection — a keep-alive or HTTP/2 loop pins one backend and would have reported a clean 20/20, hiding this entirely. Both names resolve to the SINGLE VIP 212.72.24.49, so this is not DNS round-robin between regions but per-connection load-balancing across an upstream set in which about half the endpoints are unhealthy. The same ratio reproduces on every surface of this Sovereign: console apex 10/20 served, per-Org console 11/20 (its failure mode is a TLS reset at connect, curl 35, not a 503 — the per-Org listener/cert is absent from one backend rather than merely unhealthy), openova-mcp 10/20. A customer following a voucher link therefore fails roughly every other attempt. WHAT PASSES on the connections that are served: the redeem page returns 200, title 'Redeem voucher — OpenOva', ships the 6-step funnel nav with correct hrefs (1 Plan /plans · 2 Stack /apps · 3 Add-ons /addons · 4 Topology /bcp · 5 Review /review · 6 Checkout /checkout), and carries every outcome panel server-side — redeem-missing with a manual #redeem-manual-code input + Validate, redeem-not-valid, redeem-ended, redeem-valid, plus the #5634 redeem-throttled panel that keeps a 429 rate-limit from being mis-shown as a bad code. Source, not inference: generateOpenClawHR stamps openclaw's llm.baseURL AND newapi.baseURL as https://api.<slug>.<parent>/v1 (core/services/provisioning/gitops/helmrelease_apps.go), and that host is EXACTLY the HTTPRoute hostname generateNewAPIHR gives bp-newapi in the same file. So the funnel shipped hw292's Org an openclaw whose only LLM backend is a hostname the Org never provisions — and row 225 had no per-Org bp-newapi to walk BECAUSE of that gap, not beside it. The two per-Org paths also disagreed: the BSS door emits bp-newapi.yaml for EVERY tenant Org (a fixed entry in orgTenantTemplates + orgTenantKustomization, organization_gitops.go), while the funnel gated it on the cart — #4739 added newapi to helmReleaseAppSlugs FOR 'row225 funnel parity with the BSS-door orgTenantBPNewAPI', and cart-gating is not parity. FIX: impliedHelmReleaseApps (openclaw implies newapi) applied inside helmReleaseAppsFor(), the single enumeration BOTH the rendered file set (GenerateAllWithAppConfigs) and the kustomization index set (PerOrgHostHelmReleaseAppDocs) now read from, so a rendered-but-unindexed file cannot occur. The closure is narrow (only a slug whose own values name another slug's host), duplicate-safe and order-stable. NEXT WALKER: an Org carting openclaw must show a per-Org bp-newapi HelmRelease reaching Ready with newapi-bp-newapi-admin-promote Complete 1/1 — the hook mechanism itself is already proven green on this env at host level. Guarded by helmrelease_app_dependency_closure_test.go (3 tests; 2 fail without the closure, at both seams). NOT WALKED — repo state only. ‖ ADJUDICATED 2026-08-10 — CLAUSE UPHELD, LEFT RED, and the "blueprint does not support a per-Org newapi" reading is WITHDRAWN as the reason. Two different mechanisms were being conflated. The Application-CR path resolves placement from platform/newapi/blueprint.yaml:259-261 (defaultPlacement.vcluster: mgmt, allowedPlacements host+mgmt) and therefore renders into a `mgmt` namespace no fresh prov has — that is the 201-then-Degraded experiment. The FUNNEL path never reads those fields at all: core/services/provisioning/gitops/helmrelease_apps.go:396-465 generateNewAPIHR emits a HelmRelease with `namespace: <slug>` and `targetNamespace: <slug>`, i.e. a genuine per-Org instance in the Org's own namespace. So this row's subject EXISTS, the clause is falsifiable as written, and retiring it would have retired a live requirement. NEW DEFECT FOUND, PROVEN BY RENDER WITH A CONTROL (#5987): the funnel-rendered per-Org HR CANNOT install. generateNewAPIHR passes no `catalystIntegration` values, so the chart takes its defaults — catalystIntegration.enabled=true and externalSecret.enabled=true with remoteRef.key EMPTY (platform/newapi/chart/values.yaml) — and platform/newapi/chart/templates/external-secret.yaml:42 then executes its explicit `fail`. MEASURED: `helm template` on platform/newapi/chart with exactly the values generateNewAPIHR stamps, with --api-versions external-secrets.io/v1beta1 so the chart's CRD gate is satisfied the way it is on a real host cluster, exits 1 with "catalystIntegration.externalSecret.remoteRef.key is empty". CONTROL: the same chart, same command, same api-versions, with ONLY the BSS door's catalystIntegration block added (organization_gitops.go stamps remoteRef.key sovereign/<fqdn>/newapi/<tenant>/admin-token) renders 1548 lines and exits 0 — so the failure is attributable to that one missing value and not to the invocation. CONSEQUENCE: the impliedHelmReleaseApps fix recorded above (openclaw implies newapi) now makes EVERY openclaw Org render a per-Org bp-newapi that will go InstallFailed on a template error. Stays ❌ — unchanged verdict, but the precondition story is replaced by a named source cause and fix site. ‖ RE-TAGGED 2026-08-11: NEEDS-CODE -> DEPLOY-GATED — the #5987 cause this cell names and proves by `helm template` was fixed the same day. It shipped as `93d824ea4` (2026-08-10T12:34:35Z, `the per-Org bp-newapi HelmRelease could not install, and once it could it installed empty`) and is an ancestor of `origin/main`. (1) curl https://wordpress.hw293walkone.omani.homes/ → 302 → /wp-admin/install.php → 200, title 'WordPress › Installation'; identically for https://wordpress.hw293walktwo.omani.trade/. (2) mysql pods mysql-...-x-hw293walkone-x-vcluster and mysql-...-x-hw293walktwo-x-vcluster are both QoS=Guaranteed and Running. (3) HTTPRoute app-wordpress-hostroute exists in each Org namespace, parents Gateway kube-system/cilium-gateway-console, Accepted=True and ResolvedRefs=True. (4) both hosts resolve to 212.72.24.49, the consoleLoadBalancerIP, NOT the primary ELB 212.72.24.74. WHAT FAILS: 12 FRESH TCP connections to wordpress.hw293walkone.omani.homes gave 6 x 302 and 6 x TLS connection-reset-by-peer — half of a paying customer's first loads do not complete a handshake. MECHANISM, read from both regions' live objects: the console Gateway in region A declares TEN listeners including console-https-hw293walkone / console-http-hw293walkone / console-https-hw293walktwo / console-http-hw293walktwo, while the SAME Gateway in region B declares only SIX — the four per-Org listeners were never written there — and region B holds ZERO Org HTTPRoutes. Both regions sit behind the one EIP, so the half that lands on B has no SNI match and resets. CONTROL that isolates it to the missing listener rather than to the EIP: console.hw293.omantel.biz, whose listener DOES exist in both regions, completes TLS on the same EIP and fails differently — 6 of 12 return HTTP 503 'no healthy upstream'. Listener present → 503; listener absent → reset. THIS IS NOT DEPLOY-GATED, AND THAT IS THE NEW FINDING. The #5930 fan-out (4ca4c34b4, 'per-Org console listeners span every region the ELB pool does') IS an ancestor of the deployed catalyst-api 70f6b07, checked with merge-base. It runs and reports success anyway: catalyst-api logs, every 5 minutes, 'org-console-tls: ensured per-Org console TLS trio — listener pair admitted in every region' with the fan-out's own field regions="host" — ONE pseudo-region, never me-east-215-b. Cause, traced to the seam: orgConsoleTLSPoolRegions() (org_console_tls.go:389) builds the pool region set from the on-disk secondary kubeconfigs under CATALYST_KUBECONFIGS_DIR, and inside the Sovereign's own catalyst-api pod /var/lib/catalyst/kubeconfigs is EMPTY — the mothership holds both regions' kubeconfigs, the Sovereign holds neither. len(keys)==0 returns nil, so the pool is empty, so `unreached` stays empty too and the loud 'NO client could be built for it' error never fires. Then the admission read-back loops over the SAME truncated target list, which is why it can report 'admitted in every region' having checked one: a guard iterating the set it is meant to validate cannot detect a set that lost a member. Config, for the record: ConfigMap catalyst-system/sovereign-fqdn declares configuredRegions hw-me-east-215-a-rtz-prod,hw-me-east-215-b-rtz-prod and replicaRegion hw-me-east-215-b-rtz-prod, so the second region is DECLARED and simply unreachable to this writer. Same defect family as #5511/#5246, reproduced with the remedy running. ‖ RE-TAGGED 2026-08-11: NEEDS-CODE -> DEPLOY-GATED — the seam this walk traced now HAS a merged fix, and it is in the same file. The walk was right that #5930 was merged-and-deployed-and-still-failing, and it located the cause precisely at `orgConsoleTLSPoolRegions` in `org_console_tls.go` reading an empty kubeconfigs dir. #6024 (`22ebec37d`, 2026-08-10T16:00:11Z) is that repair: it touches `products/catalyst/bootstrap/api/internal/handler/org_console_tls.go`, moves secondary-kubeconfig delivery into a level-triggered `runSecondaryKubeconfigDelivery` reached for EVERY multi-region deployment whatever Phase 1 concluded (hw293's Phase 1 latched failed on one HelmRelease, which is what starved both previous producers), and makes placement refuse to answer from a cache narrower than the declared region set — closing the guard-iterates-its-own-truncated-set defect this cell describes. It is an ancestor of `origin/main` and NOT an ancestor of the deployed `70f6b07`. Closes on a roll.
